972: func (r *Runtime) setStagedConfigMapNodeOwnerReferences(
973: 	ctx context.Context,
974: 	namespace string,
975: 	configMaps []stagedConfigMap,
976: 	nodes map[string]*unstructured.Unstructured,
977: 	maxWorkers uint,
978: ) error {
979: 	if len(configMaps) == 0 {
980: 		return nil
981: 	}
982: 
983: 	workers, _ := r.kubernetesWorkers(maxWorkers)
984: 
985: 	return runWithWorkers(configMaps, workers, func(staged stagedConfigMap) error {
986: 		nodeNames := make(map[string]struct{}, len(staged.mounts))
987: 		for _, mount := range staged.mounts {
988: 			nodeNames[mount.nodeName] = struct{}{}
989: 		}
990: 		sortedNodeNames := make([]string, 0, len(nodeNames))
991: 		for nodeName := range nodeNames {
992: 			sortedNodeNames = append(sortedNodeNames, nodeName)
993: 		}
994: 		sort.Strings(sortedNodeNames)
995: 
996: 		ownerReferences := make([]metav1.OwnerReference, 0, len(sortedNodeNames))
997: 		topologyName := ""
998: 		for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames {
999: 			node := nodes[nodeName]
1000: 			if node == nil {
1001: 				return fmt.Errorf("failed to find c9s node %s/%s for staged ConfigMap ownership",
1002: 					namespace, nodeName)
1003: 			}
1004: 			topologyName = node.GetLabels()[labelTopologyOwner]
1005: 			ownerReferences = append(ownerReferences, metav1.OwnerReference{
1006: 				APIVersion: c9sAPIVersion,
1007: 				Kind:       "Node",
1008: 				Name:       node.GetName(),
1009: 				UID:        node.GetUID(),
1010: 			})
1011: 		}
1012: 
1013: 		configMap, err := r.kubeClient.CoreV1().ConfigMaps(namespace).
1014: 			Get(ctx, staged.name, metav1.GetOptions{})
1015: 		if apierrors.IsNotFound(err) {
1016: 			configMap = stagedConfigMapObject(
1017: 				namespace,
1018: 				topologyName,
1019: 				staged,
1020: 				ownerReferences,
1021: 			)
1022: 			if _, err = r.kubeClient.CoreV1().ConfigMaps(namespace).
1023: 				Create(ctx, configMap, metav1.CreateOptions{}); err != nil {
1024: 				return fmt.Errorf(
1025: 					"failed to recreate staged ConfigMap %s/%s with owner references: %w",
1026: 					namespace,
1027: 					staged.name,
1028: 					err,
1029: 				)
1030: 			}
1031: 
1032: 			return nil
1033: 		}
1034: 		if err != nil {
1035: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to get staged ConfigMap %s/%s for owner update: %w",
1036: 				namespace,
1037: 				staged.name,
1038: 				err,
1039: 			)
1040: 		}
1041: 
1042: 		if apiequality.Semantic.DeepEqual(configMap.OwnerReferences, ownerReferences) {
1043: 			return nil
1044: 		}
1045: 		configMap.OwnerReferences = ownerReferences
1046: 
1047: 		if _, err = r.kubeClient.CoreV1().ConfigMaps(namespace).
1048: 			Update(ctx, configMap, metav1.UpdateOptions{}); err != nil {
1049: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to update staged ConfigMap %s/%s owner references: %w",
1050: 				namespace,
1051: 				staged.name,
1052: 				err,
1053: 			)
1054: 		}
1055: 
1056: 		return nil
1057: 	})
1058: }

1060: func (r *Runtime) deleteStagedConfigMaps(

1062: 	namespace string,
1063: 	configMaps []stagedConfigMap,
1064: ) {

1070: 				"namespace", namespace,

1073: 			)
1074: 		}

1078: func (r *Runtime) deleteStaleConfigMaps(
1079: 	ctx context.Context,
1080: 	namespace,
1081: 	topologyName string,
1082: 	desired []stagedConfigMap,
1083: ) error {
1084: 	desiredNames := make(map[string]struct{}, len(desired))
1085: 	for _, staged := range desired {
1086: 		desiredNames[staged.name] = struct{}{}
1087: 	}
1088: 
1089: 	configMaps, err := r.kubeClient.CoreV1().ConfigMaps(namespace).List(
1090: 		ctx,
1091: 		metav1.ListOptions{LabelSelector: labelTopologyOwner + "=" + topologyName},
1092: 	)
1093: 	if err != nil {
1094: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to list staged ConfigMaps for c9s lab %s/%s: %w",
1095: 			namespace, topologyName, err)
1096: 	}
1097: 
1098: 	for idx := range configMaps.Items {
1099: 		name := configMaps.Items[idx].Name
1100: 		if _, keep := desiredNames[name]; keep {
1101: 			continue
1102: 		}
1103: 		if err := r.kubeClient.CoreV1().ConfigMaps(namespace).
1104: 			Delete(ctx, name, metav1.DeleteOptions{}); err != nil && !apierrors.IsNotFound(err) {
1105: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed to delete stale staged ConfigMap %s/%s: %w",
1106: 				namespace, name, err)
1107: 		}
1108: 	}
1109: 
1110: 	return nil
1111: }
1112: 
1113: func safeKubernetesName(parts ...string) string {
1114: 	name := strings.Join(parts, "-")
1115: 	if len(name) > kubernetesNameMaxLen {
1116: 		digest := sha256.Sum256([]byte(name))
1117: 		name = name[0:kubernetesNameMaxLen-8] + "-" + hex.EncodeToString(digest[:])[0:7]
1118: 	}
1119: 
1120: 	name = strings.ToLower(name)
1121: 	name = invalidDNSLabelChars.ReplaceAllString(name, "-")
1122: 	name = startsWithNonAlpha.ReplaceAllString(name, "z")
1123: 	name = endsWithNonAlpha.ReplaceAllString(name, "z")
1124: 
1125: 	return name
1126: }

*Runtime) applyStagedConfigMaps(\n874: \tctx context.Context,\n875: \tnamespace string,\n876: \ttopologyName string,\n877: \tconfigMaps []stagedConfigMap,\n878: \tmaxWorkers uint,\n879: ) error stagedConfigMapsConform(existing, desired *corev1.ConfigMap) bool {\n935: \treturn apiequality.Semantic.DeepEqual(existing.Labels, desired.Labels) &&\n936: \t\tapiequality.Semantic.DeepEqual(existing.Data, desired.Data) &&\n937: \t\tapiequality.Semantic.DeepEqual(existing.BinaryData, desired.BinaryData)\n938: }\n939: \n940: func Reasoning
OK. So clabernetes' own staging (`replaceClabPathVariables`) already substitutes magic vars for startup-config, license, binds (via `stageSourcePathIntoConfigMaps` → `resolveLocalFiles` which calls `replaceClabPathVariables`), env-files, copy-to-flash. So the PR adds expansion at the CLI side too.

Now, important interplay: after this PR, `c.renderedTopology` (as passed in DeployRequest.TopologyDefinition) has magic vars expanded per node. But clabernetes staging uses `GetNodeBinds(nodeName)` etc. from this parsed definition, and calls `replaceClabPathVariables` again — idempotent since no magic vars remain. OK.

Now key correctness questions for the new code:

1. **`processNodeExecs`** — let me check it in config.go lines 994-1030.

2. **`GetNodeBinds`** — check whether it errors when bind parsing fails (`NewBindFromString`). Also whether the returned binds are merged from defaults/kinds/groups.

3. **Double parse**: `source` and `expanded` are identical unmarshals of the same bytes. `source` is read-only; `expanded` gets mutated. But `expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars` reads from `source` and writes to `expanded`. Since both start identical, one unmarshal could serve both purposes if a deep copy were made... Actually no — they need two separate objects so that mutation of `expanded` doesn't corrupt `source`. The unmarshal of the same bytes into two structs is the copy mechanism. But yaml.Unmarshal is called twice on the same bytes — could be done once into `expanded` and then deep-copy? yaml.v2 doesn't have a deep copy. Alternatively, could unmarshal once into `source`, then for `expanded` re-unmarshal — that's what they do. Actually it's needed since NodeDefinition maps hold pointers. Fine; two unmarshals are justified, though one could avoid it. Actually there is a subtle aliasing bug risk: if `yaml.Unmarshal` into `expanded` and `source` from the same bytes, and `expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars` reads `source` — fine.

Wait, actually there's a subtler issue: `expanded.Nodes[nodeName]` — for nodes that are defined, expanded.Nodes[nodeName] is non-nil. The mutation of the *existing* NodeDefinition in `expanded` (from Unmarshal) is fine.

But here's a real issue: `node.Stages` — `GetStages(nodeName)` returns merged stages from defaults/kinds/groups/node. Then `clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields` clears `Stages` from defaults/kinds/groups, so the merged stages only live on nodes. But the Stages struct contains pointers to maps? If `GetStages` shares sub-objects between the source's definitions... wait, `source.GetStages` — the stages merge likely creates copies. Let me check GetStages in types/topology.go.

Another issue: `processNodeExecs` is applied on node exec and stage execs. What does it do — magic var replacement plus something else? Check lines 994-1022.

4. **The `Config` struct in core** — check what it is (maybe defined in core/config.go) and whether re-marshaling `expanded.Topology` (a `*clabtypes.Topology`) loses fields. The original `topology:` section keys are dictated by the `Topology` struct's yaml tags when re-marshaled. Unknown keys in the topology section (not part of the struct) would be dropped — same behavior as the existing clabernetes renderer path when definition changed. But the pre-PR path (deploy without this expansion... wait, does base have this? No — this is the PR adding it). Base: `renderedTopology` was passed through raw. So any YAML in the topology file that `clabtypes.Topology` doesn't model but clabernetes' parser (`clabRuntimeConfig`/`clabernetesRenderTopology`) understands... they're the same model. What about top-level keys? The MapSlice approach preserves top-level keys like `name`, `mgmt`, `settings` as raw YAML. But the `topology` value replacement re-marshals through `clabtypes.Topology`, dropping unknown keys *within* topology. Also, ordering within topology changes (struct field order, not file order). Cosmetic.

More important: **anchors/aliases**. If the topology YAML uses YAML anchors (`&`/`*`) or merge keys `<<:`, unmarshaling into structs and re-marshaling expands them — usually fine.

But wait — does `renderedTopology` at this point even contain anchors? It's the raw file content after env var substitution? Let me check `core/file.go` line 87: `c.renderedTopology = append(c.renderedTopology[:0], yamlFile...)`. Let me look at file.go to see what rendering happens.

5. **The MapSlice loop bug**: `for i := range document { if document[i].Key == "topology" { ... } }` — if there's no "topology" key (topology is required though), nothing replaced. Fine.

But there's a subtle issue: `document[i].Value = expanded.Topology` — `expanded.Topology` is `*clabtypes.Topology`. yaml.Marshal of the whole MapSlice: top-level keys parsed as `interface{}` from yaml — a `name: lab1` key will be `string`. Fine.

However: yaml.v2 `Marshal` on `clabtypes.Topology` — need to check that `Topology` has yaml tags matching what the runtime expects (it does, it's the same struct used elsewhere).

But hold on — a real correctness concern: **yaml.v2 map ordering**. When marshaling `expanded.Topology`, the `Nodes map[string]*NodeDefinition` will be marshaled... yaml.v2 marshals maps sorted by key? Yes, yaml.v2 sorts map keys. OK deterministic.

6. **`c.Config.Topology` mutation**: The test asserts source topology in `c.Config.Topology` was NOT modified (`Defaults.StartupConfig` still has `__clabNodeName__`). Good — but note the new code mutates `c.renderedTopology`, replacing it with expanded bytes. Is `c.renderedTopology` used elsewhere after this where rawness matters? Grep showed uses in labruntime.go (deploy request) and file.go (set). Also does `parseTopology` set `c.Config` from `renderedTopology`? If `parseTopology` runs after `prepareLabRuntimeTopology`... Let me check the call order: in `NewContainerLab`, options run, then `c.parseTopology()` at line 120? Actually at line 120 in clab.go: `err = c.parseTopology()`. Where is `prepareLabRuntimeTopology` called? Let me grep.

7. **`labRuntimeNodeHasStages` guard**: If `source.GetStages` returns merged stages, why guard? Maybe GetStages errors when no stages found? Let me check.

8. **`node.KindSpecificConfig`**: `source.GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName)` returns merged entries from kinds/groups/defaults/node presumably. Then `clearInheritedLabRuntimeNodeFields` clears `KindSpecificConfig` from defaults/kinds/groups. But **nodes' own KindSpecificConfig remains in expanded** — and it was already replaced with the expanded entries (merged). Fine. But note: if `len(entries) != 0`, they overwrite node.KindSpecificConfig with merged map; if `len(entries) == 0`, node.KindSpecificConfig stays as-is (empty). OK.

But wait — there's a bug potential: `GetNodeKindSpecificConfig` — check signature: returns `[]KindSpecificConfigEntry`. If entries include the node's own keys, fine.

9. **Critical check — `node.Exec = source.GetNodeExec(nodeName)` then `processNodeExecs(nodeConfig)`**: `processNodeExecs` mutates `nodeCfg.Exec`? Let me check its implementation. In config.go line 994-1022. It probably iterates over exec strings and replaces magic vars using `nodeCfg.ShortName`. But the NodeConfig created here only has ShortName, Exec, Stages — does processNodeExecs need other fields (e.g., TopoPaths)? It's a method on c, so TopoPaths is available. Let me read it.

Reasoning
Note: `processNodeExecs` copies each exec (`execCopy := *exec`) — so it doesn't mutate shared exec pointers in `nodeCfg.Stages`... wait it does replace the slice entries with copies, but `GetStages` may return shared pointers into the `source` topology? Actually the NodeConfig here is built fresh, with `Stages: node.Stages` from `source.GetStages(nodeName)`. Let me check `GetStages` — if it returns a merged struct that shares `*Exec` pointers with `source` definitions, `processNodeExecs` copies them, good. But the stages' Exec slices in `source` — `execCopy := *exec; stage.Execs[i] = &execCopy` mutates the slice, which may be shared with source. Hmm, but processNodeExecs replaces elements of the slice `stage.Execs` — if the slice is shared with the source topology's stages, then the source's exec strings get replaced in place (slice element overwrite). Wait but the test asserts source topology (c.Config.Topology.Defaults.StartupConfig) was NOT modified — that checks `c.Config.Topology`, which is parsed from `c.renderedTopology` separately? Actually `c.Config` is built from the file at parse time, separate objects from `source`/`expanded`. The test's assertion is about `c.Config.Topology`, not `source`. But `processNodeExecs` mutating `source`'s shared slices wouldn't matter for `c.Config.Topology`. Hmm, but it could matter for repeated expansion across nodes: `nodeConfig.Stages` from `source.GetStages(nodeName)` — if `GetStages` returns shared slices across nodes (e.g., both r1 and r2 inherit the same slice from defaults), then processing r1 replaces elements in the shared slice, so when r2's GetStages returns the same underlying slice, r1's replacements are already baked in — but since r2's replacement is by node name, and magicVarReplacer for r2 would replace `__clabNodeName__` — if r1 already replaced them, r2 gets r1's values! That's a real cross-node contamination bug IF GetStages shares slices.

Let me check GetStages in types/topology.go to see whether it copies. Also check whether stages' Exec slices come from shared pointers.

Similarly, `source.GetNodeExec(nodeName)` — if it returns the merged slice shared with `topology.Defaults.Exec`, processing r1 would replace elements in place; then r2 calling GetNodeExec would... let me check. Typically these getters build new slices. Let me read types/topology.go relevant functions.

893: // GetNodeVolumes merges volume entries from defaults, kind, group, and node levels.
894: // Lower-level entries override higher-level ones for the same destination path.
895: func (t *Topology) GetNodeVolumes(name string) ([]string, error) {
896: 	volumeSources := mergeStringSliceFields(
897: 		t,
898: 		name,
899: 		getNodeVolumeSources,
900: 		getNodeVolumeSources,
901: 		getNodeVolumeSources,
902: 		getNodeVolumeSources,
903: 	)
904: 
905: 	volumes := map[string]string{}
906: 
907: 	// add the volumes from less to more specific levels, indexed by the destination path.
908: 	// thereby more specific volumes will overwrite less specific ones
909: 	for _, volume := range volumeSources {
910: 		v, err := NewVolumeFromString(volume)
911: 		if err != nil {
912: 			return nil, err
913: 		}
914: 		volumes[v.Dst()] = volume
915: 	}
916: 
917: 	// in order to return nil instead of empty array when no volumes are defined
918: 	if len(volumes) == 0 {
919: 		return nil, nil
920: 	}
921: 
922: 	// build the result array with all the entries from volumes map
923: 	result := make([]string, 0, len(volumes))
924: 
925: 	for _, v := range volumes {
926: 		result = append(result, v)
927: 	}
928: 
929: 	slices.Sort(result)
930: 
931: 	return result, nil
932: }

961: // GetStages return the configuration stages set for the given node.
962: // It merges the default, kind and node stages into a single Stages struct
963: // with node stages taking precedence over kind stages and default stages.
964: func (t *Topology) GetStages(nodeName string) (*Stages, error) {
965: 	s := NewStages()
966:

968: 	if defaultStages != nil {
969: 		s.Merge(defaultStages)
970: 	}

974: 		s.Merge(kind.Stages)
975: 	}
976:

978: 	if group != nil && group.Stages != nil {
979: 		s.Merge(group.Stages)
980: 	}

984: 		s.Merge(nodeDefinition.Stages)
985: 	}
986: 
987: 	// set nil values to their respective defaults
988: 	s.InitDefaults()

1006: 	for _, g := range t.Groups {
1007: 		g.ImportEnvsFrom(env)
1008: 	}
1009: 
1010: 	for _, n := range t.Nodes {
1011: 		n.ImportEnvsFrom(env)
1012: 	}
1013: }

1015: // GetCertificateConfig returns the certificate configuration for the given node.
1016: func (t *Topology) GetCertificateConfig(nodeName string) *CertificateConfig {
1017: 	// default for issuing node certificates is false
1018: 	cc := &CertificateConfig{
1019: 		Issue: new(false),
1020: 	}
1021: 
1022: 	cc.Merge(t.GetDefaults().Certificate)
1023:

1035: 	if nodeDefinition != nil {

1051: // credentialsPresent reports whether a credentials block supplies a username/password pair. The
1052: // identity-file is resolved on its own precedence chain (resolveTopologyIdentityFile) and is
1053: // intentionally excluded here so it does not influence the username/password winning level used by
1054: // inventory generation.
1055: func credentialsPresent(c NodeCredentials) bool {
1056: 	return c.Username != "" || c.Password != ""
1057: }
1058: 
1059: // resolveTopologyCredentials returns username and password from the most specific topology level
1060: // where either field is set (node, then group, then kind, then defaults). Values are not merged
1061: // across levels.
1062: func (t *Topology) resolveTopologyCredentials(
1063: 	nodeName string,
1064: ) (username, password string, src CredentialTopologySource) {
1065: 	nodeDefinition, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]
1066: 	if !ok {
1067: 		return "", "", CredentialTopologyUnset
1068: 	}
1069: 
1070: 	if nodeDefinition != nil && credentialsPresent(nodeDefinition.Credentials) {
1071: 		c := nodeDefinition.Credentials
1072: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyNode
1073: 	}
1074: 
1075: 	group := t.GetGroup(t.GetNodeGroup(nodeName))
1076: 	if group != nil && credentialsPresent(group.Credentials) {
1077: 		c := group.Credentials
1078: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyGroup
1079: 	}
1080: 
1081: 	kind := t.GetKind(t.GetNodeKind(nodeName))
1082: 	if kind != nil && credentialsPresent(kind.Credentials) {
1083: 		c := kind.Credentials
1084: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyKind
1085: 	}
1086: 
1087: 	defaults := t.GetDefaults()
1088: 	if defaults != nil && credentialsPresent(defaults.Credentials) {
1089: 		c := defaults.Credentials
1090: 		return c.Username, c.Password, CredentialTopologyDefaults
1091: 	}
1092: 
1093: 	return "", "", CredentialTopologyUnset
1094: }
1095: 
1096: // resolveTopologyIdentityFile returns the SSH identity-file path from the most specific topology
1097: // level where it is set (node, then group, then kind, then defaults). It is resolved independently
1098: // of username/password so a node may set only identity-file and still have it apply, without
1099: // affecting the username/password winning level (see resolveTopologyCredentials).
1100: func (t *Topology) resolveTopologyIdentityFile(nodeName string) string {
1101: 	nodeDefinition, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]
1102: 	if !ok {
1103: 		return ""
1104: 	}
1105: 
1106: 	if nodeDefinition != nil && nodeDefinition.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1107: 		return nodeDefinition.Credentials.IdentityFile
1108: 	}
1109: 
1110: 	if group := t.GetGroup(t.GetNodeGroup(nodeName)); group != nil &&
1111: 		group.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1112: 		return group.Credentials.IdentityFile
1113: 	}
1114: 
1115: 	if kind := t.GetKind(t.GetNodeKind(nodeName)); kind != nil &&
1116: 		kind.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1117: 		return kind.Credentials.IdentityFile
1118: 	}
1119: 
1120: 	if defaults := t.GetDefaults(); defaults != nil && defaults.Credentials.IdentityFile != "" {
1121: 		return defaults.Credentials.IdentityFile
1122: 	}
1123: 
1124: 	return ""
1125: }
1126: 
1127: // GetNodeUsername returns the username from the topology credentials block at the winning
1128: // precedence level (see resolveTopologyCredentials). Empty if unset at every level.
1129: func (t *Topology) GetNodeUsername(nodeName string) string {
1130: 	u, _, _ := t.resolveTopologyCredentials(nodeName)
1131: 	return u
1132: }
1133: 
1134: // GetNodePassword returns the password from the topology credentials block at the winning
1135: // precedence level (see resolveTopologyCredentials). Empty if unset at every level.
1136: func (t *Topology) GetNodePassword(nodeName string) string {
1137: 	_, p, _ := t.resolveTopologyCredentials(nodeName)
1138: 	return p

1141: // GetNodeIdentityFile returns the SSH identity-file path from the topology credentials block at the
1142: // winning precedence level (see resolveTopologyIdentityFile). Empty if unset at every level.
1143: func (t *Topology) GetNodeIdentityFile(nodeName string) string {
1144: 	return t.resolveTopologyIdentityFile(nodeName)
1145: }

1147: // CredentialTopologySource identifies which topology level supplied the credentials block
1148: // before registry fallback. Precedence: node, then group, then kind, then defaults.
1149: type CredentialTopologySource int
1150: 
1151: const (
1152: 	// CredentialTopologyUnset means no topology level set credentials (registry may still fill
1153: 	// NodeConfig).
1154: 	CredentialTopologyUnset CredentialTopologySource = iota
1155: 	CredentialTopologyDefaults
1156: 	CredentialTopologyKind
1157: 	CredentialTopologyGroup
1158: 	CredentialTopologyNode
1159: )
1160: 
1161: // GetNodeCredentialsTopologySource reports which topology level supplied the credentials pair
1162: // (username and/or password) before registry fallback.
1163: func (t *Topology) GetNodeCredentialsTopologySource(nodeName string) CredentialTopologySource {
1164: 	_, _, src := t.resolveTopologyCredentials(nodeName)
1165: 	return src
1166: }

1168: // GetComponents returns the node's raw components kind-specific config value, or nil when unset.
1169: //
1170: // Deprecated: components are kind-specific config; use GetNodeKindSpecificConfig. Kept for
1171: // clabernetes, which
1172: // transcodes the value into its own component type.
1173: func (t *Topology) GetComponents(nodeName string) any {
1174: 	for _, e := range t.GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName) {
1175: 		if e.Key == "components" {
1176: 			return e.Value
1177: 		}
1178: 	}
1179: 
1180: 	return nil
1181: }
1182: 
1183: // KindSpecificConfigEntry is a raw kind-specific config key of a node with the topology block it
1184: // came from.
1185: type KindSpecificConfigEntry struct {
1186: 	Key   string
1187: 	Value any
1188: 	// From is the block that set the key: nodes.<name>, groups.<name>, kinds.<name> or defaults.
1189: 	From string
1190: }
1191: 
1192: // GetNodeKindSpecificConfig returns the node's raw kind-specific config keys merged with precedence
1193: // node > group > kind > defaults, sorted by key. The first block that sets a key provides its
1194: // whole value. Nodes absent from the topology have no kind-specific config.
1195: func (t *Topology) GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName string) []KindSpecificConfigEntry {
1196: 	nodeDef, ok := t.Nodes[nodeName]
1197: 	if !ok {
1198: 		return nil
1199: 	}
1200: 
1201: 	group := t.GetNodeGroup(nodeName)
1202: 	kind := t.GetNodeKind(nodeName)
1203: 
1204: 	blocks := []struct {
1205: 		from string
1206: 		def  *NodeDefinition
1207: 	}{
1208: 		{"nodes." + nodeName, nodeDef},
1209: 		{"groups." + group, t.GetGroup(group)},
1210: 		{"kinds." + kind, t.GetKind(kind)},
1211: 		{"defaults", t.GetDefaults()},
1212: 	}
1213: 
1214: 	var entries []KindSpecificConfigEntry
1215: 
1216: 	seen := map[string]bool{}
1217: 
1218: 	for _, b := range blocks {
1219: 		if b.def == nil {
1220: 			continue
1221: 		}
1222: 
1223: 		for k, v := range b.def.KindSpecificConfig {
1224: 			if seen[k] {
1225: 				continue
1226: 			}
1227: 
1228: 			seen[k] = true
1229: 
1230: 			entries = append(entries, KindSpecificConfigEntry{Key: k, Value: v, From: b.from})
1231: 		}
1232: 	}
1233: 
1234: 	slices.SortFunc(
1235: 		entries,
1236: 		func(a, b KindSpecificConfigEntry) int { return strings.Compare(a.Key, b.Key) },
1237: 	)
1238: 
1239: 	return entries

Reasoning
Key observations:

- `GetStages` merges into a fresh `NewStages()` via `s.Merge(...)`. Need to check whether `Stages.Merge` deep-copies or shares `*Exec` pointers. `processNodeExecs` copies each exec (`execCopy := *exec`), so pointer sharing into source is handled for exec commands... but it mutates `stage.Execs[i]` slice entries in the merged `s` — that's a merged struct, not the source. So no cross-node contamination via stages if Merge deep-copies slices. But `processNodeExecs` gets `nodeConfig.Stages` from `GetStages` — the merged copy. Let me check `Stages.Merge` to be sure it deep-copies `Execs` slices.

- `GetNodeBinds` returns a freshly built `result` slice — safe.

- `GetNodeVolumes` returns a new slice — safe. But note: `NewVolumeFromString(volume)` — the parsed volume; volumes map stores the original `volume` string. Fine.

- `GetNodeExec` returns `mergeStringSliceFields` result — need to check that mergeStringSliceFields builds a new slice (likely). Let me verify quickly.

Now the critical interplay: `processNodeExecs` on the NodeConfig with `ShortName: nodeName`. It replaces magic vars. But it uses `c.magicVarReplacer` which depends on `c.TopoPaths` — in `prepareLabRuntimeTopology`, `TopoPaths.SetLabDirByPrefix(c.Config.Name)` was called earlier (line 184), so lab dir is set. Good — and that's why the expansion happens after sanitize and lab dir setup. The diff places the call at the end of prepareLabRuntimeTopology. Good ordering.

Wait — but actually the test asserts `wantExec := "echo clab-lab1 " + nodeName + " " + filepath.Join(topologyDir, "clab-lab1") + ...`. So `__clabDir__` → `topologyDir/clab-lab1`. `magicVarReplacer` uses `c.TopoPaths.TopologyLabDir()`. For the test, `NewContainerLab(WithTopoPath(topologyFile, nil), WithRuntime(ClabernetesRuntimeName))` — does NewContainerLab call prepareLabRuntimeTopology? Let me check clab.go lines 80-132 and the WithTopoPath option. Line 118: `err = c.prepareLabRuntimeTopology()` inside some option. Let me read clab.go beginning.

Also check `expandKindSpecificConfigMagicVars` in kind_specific_config.go.

And check `mergeStringSliceFields`.

Also: the `document[i].Key == "topology"` replacement — since `expanded.Topology` is `*clabtypes.Topology` with all fields; when marshaled, any field with `yaml:"...,omitempty"` etc. But `Topology` may have fields not omitempty... it's the same struct used by clabernetes renderer. One nuance: `Topology.Links` — `clabtypes.Topology` has `Links []any`? Let me check the Topology struct definition to make sure the round-trip preserves links. If `Links` is `[]any` (raw), yaml.v2 unmarshals into `map[interface{}]interface{}` and re-marshals fine.

Actually wait, there's a bigger issue: `types.Topology` — does it have `Links` field with a custom type that fails to unmarshal from raw yaml? If parse of `c.renderedTopology` into `Config` succeeded for the source, the expanded round trip is the same. Fine.

But actually — the most interesting correctness question: `if source.Topology == nil { return c.renderedTopology, nil }` — a topology without `topology:` key. Fine.

Now, **does the round-trip lose data?** The MapSlice is unmarshaled from `c.renderedTopology`, then only the `topology` value is replaced. Marshaling MapSlice with interface{} values for other keys preserves them (though map values become sorted `map[interface{}]interface{}`). Top-level keys like `mgmt` remain raw. OK.

But here's the subtle loss: unknown keys *inside* `topology:` are dropped (they get unmarshaled into `clabtypes.Topology` which ignores unknown fields, then re-marshaled from the struct). Does `clabtypes.Topology` ignore unknown fields? yaml.v2 default ignores unknown fields (no UnmarshalStrict). Are there known topology keys that the struct doesn't model? E.g., `topology.settings`? `settings` is top-level. `topology.import-envs`? Let me check the Topology struct fields. Also `topology.config_vars`? Let me check.

Also: does the clabernetes runtime parse `TopologyDefinition` itself with `clabRuntimeConfig` — same struct, so no info loss for its purposes. But DeployRequest.TopologyDefinition might also be used for the topology CR (`NoTopologyCR` flag) — the same definition. So the expansion is consistent with what c9s parses.

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"errors"
5: 	"fmt"
6: 	"maps"
7: 	"slices"
8: 	"strings"
9: 
10: 	clabnodes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/nodes"
11: 	clabtypes "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/types"
12: )
13: 
14: // decodeKindSpecificConfig decodes the node's raw kind-specific config keys from topo into its
15: // kind's config type.
16: func (c *CLab) decodeKindSpecificConfig(
17: 	topo *clabtypes.Topology,
18: 	nodeName, kind string,
19: ) (any, error) {
20: 	var entry *clabnodes.NodeRegistryEntry
21: 	if c.Reg != nil {
22: 		entry = c.Reg.Kind(kind)
23: 	}
24: 
25: 	return clabnodes.DecodeKindSpecificConfig(
26: 		entry,
27: 		nodeName,
28: 		kind,
29: 		c.expandKindSpecificConfigMagicVars(
30: 			topo.GetNodeKindSpecificConfig(nodeName),
31: 			nodeName,
32: 		),
33: 	)
34: }
35: 
36: // expandKindSpecificConfigMagicVars replaces magic variables in every string of the given raw
37: // kind-specific config entries. Values are copied, so the topology definitions the entries point
38: // at are left untouched and keep serving other nodes unchanged.
39: func (c *CLab) expandKindSpecificConfigMagicVars(
40: 	entries []clabtypes.KindSpecificConfigEntry,
41: 	nodeName string,
42: ) []clabtypes.KindSpecificConfigEntry {
43: 	if c.TopoPaths == nil || !c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileIsSet() {
44: 		// Without the topology paths the magic variables cannot be resolved; the entries are
45: 		// returned as parsed.
46: 		return entries
47: 	}
48: 
49: 	r := c.magicVarReplacer(nodeName)
50: 
51: 	var expand func(any) any
52: 
53: 	expand = func(v any) any {
54: 		switch v := v.(type) {
55: 		case string:
56: 			return r.Replace(v)
57: 		case []any:
58: 			out := make([]any, len(v))
59: 			for i, e := range v {
60: 				out[i] = expand(e)
61: 			}
62: 
63: 			return out
64: 		case map[string]any:
65: 			out := make(map[string]any, len(v))
66: 			for k, e := range v {
67: 				out[k] = expand(e)
68: 			}
69: 
70: 			return out
71: 		case map[any]any:
72: 			out := make(map[any]any, len(v))
73: 			for k, e := range v {
74: 				out[expand(k)] = expand(e)
75: 			}
76: 
77: 			return out
78: 		default:
79: 			return v
80: 		}
81: 	}
82: 
83: 	out := make([]clabtypes.KindSpecificConfigEntry, len(entries))
84: 	for i, e := range entries {
85: 		out[i] = e
86: 		out[i].Value = expand(e.Value)
87: 	}
88: 
89: 	return out
90: }
91: 
92: // validateKindSpecificConfigKeys checks the kind-specific config keys of the defaults, kinds and
93: // groups blocks,
94: // including blocks no node uses: keys under kinds.<kind> must belong to that kind, keys of a
95: // defaults or group block that sets a kind must belong to that kind, and every other key must
96: // belong to at least one kind. Node blocks are checked when each node's kind-specific config is
97: // decoded.
98: func (c *CLab) validateKindSpecificConfigKeys() error {
99: 	if c.Reg == nil {
100: 		return nil
101: 	}
102: 
103: 	topo := c.Config.Topology
104: 
105: 	var errs []error
106: 
107: 	check := func(from, kind string, def *clabtypes.NodeDefinition) {
108: 		if def == nil {
109: 			return
110: 		}
111: 
112: 		for _, key := range slices.Sorted(maps.Keys(def.KindSpecificConfig)) {
113: 			switch {
114: 			case kind != "":
115: 				if !c.Reg.Kind(kind).AcceptsKindSpecificConfigKey(key) {
116: 					errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("kind %q does not support key %q (set in %s)",
117: 						kind, key, from))
118: 				}
119: 			case !c.anyKindAcceptsKindSpecificConfigKey(key):
120: 				errs = append(errs, fmt.Errorf("no kind supports key %q (set in %s)", key, from))
121: 			}
122: 		}
123: 	}
124: 
125: 	check("defaults", strings.ToLower(topo.GetDefaults().Kind), topo.GetDefaults())
126: 
127: 	for _, name := range slices.Sorted(maps.Keys(topo.Kinds)) {
128: 		check("kinds."+name, strings.ToLower(name), topo.Kinds[name])
129: 	}
130: 
131: 	for _, name := range slices.Sorted(maps.Keys(topo.Groups)) {
132: 		if def := topo.Groups[name]; def != nil {
133: 			check("groups."+name, strings.ToLower(def.Kind), def)
134: 		}
135: 	}
136: 
137: 	return errors.Join(errs...)
138: }
139: 
140: func (c *CLab) anyKindAcceptsKindSpecificConfigKey(key string) bool {
141: 	for _, kind := range c.Reg.GetRegisteredNodeKindNames() {
142: 		if c.Reg.Kind(kind).AcceptsKindSpecificConfigKey(key) {
143: 			return true
144: 		}
145: 	}
146: 
147: 	return false
148: }

92: // Merge merges stage other into stage s.

268: // StageBase represents a common configuration stage.
269: // Other stages embed this type to inherit its configuration options.
270: type StageBase struct {
271: 	WaitFor WaitForList `yaml:"wait-for,omitempty"`
272: 	Execs   Execs       `yaml:"exec,omitempty"`
273: }
274: 
275: // WaitForList is a list of WaitFor configurations.
276: type WaitForList []*WaitFor
277: 
278: // contains returns true if the WaitForList contains the given WaitFor.
279: func (wfl WaitForList) contains(wf *WaitFor) bool {
280: 	for _, entry := range wfl {
281: 		if entry.Equals(wf) {
282: 			return true
283: 		}
284: 	}
285: 
286: 	return false
287: }
288: 
289: // Merge merges base stage from sc into s.
290: // Merging for WaitFor and Exec commands is done by appending from sc to s without duplicates.
291: func (s *StageBase) Merge(sb *StageBase) error {
292: 	if sb == nil {
293: 		return nil
294: 	}
295: 
296: 	for _, wf := range sb.WaitFor {
297: 		// prevent adding the same dependency twice
298: 		if s.WaitFor.contains(wf) {
299: 			continue
300: 		}
301: 
302: 		s.WaitFor = append(s.WaitFor, wf)
303: 	}
304: 
305: 	s.Execs = append(s.Execs, sb.Execs...)
306: 
307: 	return nil
308: }
309: 
310: // WaitForStage defines the stages that nodes go through
311: // during the deployment process. They are used to define and enforce
312: // dependencies between nodes.
313: type WaitForStage string
314: 
315: // WaitFor represents the wait-for configuration for a node deployment stage.
316: type WaitFor struct {
317: 	Node  string       `json:"node"`            // the node that is to be waited for
318: 	Stage WaitForStage `json:"stage,omitempty"` // the stage that the node must have completed
319: }
320: 
321: // Equals returns true if the Node and the State of the WaitFor structs are value equal.
322: func (w *WaitFor) Equals(other *WaitFor) bool {
323: 	if w.Node == other.Node && w.Stage == other.Stage {
324: 		return true
325: 	}
326: 
327: 	return false
328: }
329: 
330: // GetWaitForStages returns list of wait for stages that are used to init Waitgroups
331: // for all the states.
332: func GetWaitForStages() []WaitForStage {
333: 	return []WaitForStage{
334: 		WaitForCreate, WaitForCreateLinks,
335: 		WaitForConfigure, WaitForHealthy, WaitForExit,
336: 	}

Reasoning
Very important finding: `StageBase.Merge` does `s.Execs = append(s.Execs, sb.Execs...)` — it appends **the same `*Exec` pointers** from the source definitions into the merged Stages. So `GetStages(nodeName)` for node r1 returns a Stages whose `Configure.Execs` slice may contain pointers shared with `source.Topology.Defaults.Stages.Configure.Execs` entries.

Now in `expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars`, `processNodeExecs(nodeConfig)` handles this by copying each exec (`execCopy := *exec; stage.Execs[i] = &execCopy`) — the pointer is replaced in the merged slice, not the source. So source's `*Exec` objects are not mutated.

Wait, but there's still a subtle issue: `stage.Execs[i] = &execCopy` — the slice `stage.Execs` is the merged Stages' slice (fresh from NewStages + appends), not shared with source. Good.

But: `WaitFor` pointers ARE shared (`s.WaitFor = append(s.WaitFor, wf)`) — but processNodeExecs doesn't touch WaitFor. Fine.

However — a REAL issue with `labRuntimeNodeHasStages` guard: if `labRuntimeNodeHasStages` returns false, `node.Stages` remains nil. Good. If true, `GetStages` returns merged with InitDefaults — all five stages non-nil with empty exec lists. That gets serialized to the expanded topology for ALL nodes whenever ANY level has stages. Hmm, that means nodes without stages get empty stages blocks (`create: {}, create-links: {}, configure: {}, healthy: {}, exit: {}`) in the emitted YAML. Is that harmful? The clabernetes runtime will parse and process them — with `Execs{}` empty. Probably semantically equivalent to no stages? Actually, in the local path, nodes with no stages defined get `InitDefaults` applied too (default phases). Hmm, but consider a node that explicitly relies on... it's the same behavior as local. Probably fine, just noise. Not a finding.

Wait, actually there IS a potential real correctness issue with the stage exec expansion: when stages come from the group/kind/defaults and contain magic vars, `GetStages` merges into new Stages per node — each node gets fresh copies of the Execs *slice* but shared *Exec pointers... no wait, `append(s.Execs, sb.Execs...)` copies the pointers into s.Execs (a new backing array owned by s). Then processNodeExecs replaces elements with copies. So no cross-node contamination. Good.

Hmm wait, but actually one more: `processNodeExecs` is called with `nodeConfig` where `Stages: node.Stages` — that's the merged stages from `GetStages`. Fine.

Now the `mergeStringSliceFields` uses `clabutils.MergeStringSlices(defaultsField, fieldKind, fieldGroup, fieldNode)` — check whether it produces a new slice. Let me check utils.MergeStringSlices. Likely returns new slice. GetNodeExec returns that — safe to mutate? `processNodeExecs` mutates elements of nodeConfig.Exec in place (nodeCfg.Exec[i] = r.Replace(e)) — node.Exec = source.GetNodeExec(...) — new slice, safe.

Now the bigger-picture checks:

**A. Duplication with the existing per-node processing path.** In the local (`parseTopology`) flow, magic vars in binds/volumes/exec/startup-config are handled by `NewNodeConfig` (config.go ~line 460-500) via `resolveBindPaths`/`resolveVolumePaths`/`processStartupConfig`/`processNodeExecs`, and kind-specific config via `decodeKindSpecificConfig`. The new code reimplements a subset of that for the lab-runtime deploy request. This is somewhat inherent since the local path works on NodeConfig, not on YAML. OK.

**B. The `c.renderedTopology` overwrite affects all lab-runtime consumers:** `deployWithLabRuntime`, `LabRuntimeManifests`, `ValidateLabRuntimeTopology` — all get the expanded definition. That seems to be the intent ("renders the per-node values that local runtimes normally resolve").

**C. Path resolution difference:** `resolveBindPaths` resolves host path to absolute path (`clabutils.ResolvePath(hp, c.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir())`) and validates existence. The new code only replaces magic vars but leaves relative paths relative and doesn't validate. For clabernetes, `stageBindFiles` → `stageSourcePathIntoConfigMaps` → `resolveLocalFiles` resolves relative to `topologyFileDir` — consistent. And `replaceClabPathVariables` inside c9s handles magic vars anyway. So no regression.

But wait — there's now a *double* expansion for clabernetes: c9s's `replaceClabPathVariables` still runs on the (already-expanded) definition — no magic vars remain, so idempotent. OK.

**D. Volumes handling inconsistency:** For binds, `parts[0] = r.Replace(parts[0])` regardless of len(parts); for volumes, skip when len(parts)==1. Mirrors existing `resolveBindPaths` (always replaces elems[0]) and `resolveVolumePaths` (skip when len==1). Hmm, resolveBindPaths also *resolves* the path. Consistency with existing code is a reasonable justification. Not a finding.

**E. Windows path in binds:** `strings.Split(bind, ":")` — a Windows-style bind `C:\path:/target` would split into ["C", "\\path", "/target"]; replacing parts[0]="C" is harmless (no magic var match). But existing code has the same behavior. Not a new issue.

**F. The `document[i].Key == "topology"` comparison and MapSlice:** If the YAML file has `topology:` key, fine. MapSlice preserves other top-level keys. But **unknown top-level keys are preserved** (good) while unknown keys inside `topology:` are dropped. What topology-level keys exist in `clabtypes.Topology` but not marshaled back? Let me check the Topology struct. Also there's `Config.Mgmt` with `yaml:"-"` — meaning when unmarshaling `c.renderedTopology` into `core.Config`, `mgmt` is NOT read into `Config.Mgmt`! But the MapSlice keeps the raw `mgmt` key, so the output YAML retains `mgmt`. OK good — but note `Config.MgmtNetworks` has `yaml:"mgmt,omitempty"`. So if the source has `mgmt: {...}`, unmarshaling into Config populates `MgmtNetworks`... but the MapSlice replaces only the "topology" item; `mgmt` stays raw. So `expanded.MgmtNetworks` is unused. OK.

Wait — actually there's a subtle bug candidate here: the MapSlice document is parsed from `c.renderedTopology`. Then `document[i].Value = expanded.Topology`. `expanded.Topology` is `*clabtypes.Topology`. When yaml.Marshal processes the MapItem value `*clabtypes.Topology` — will it marshal using yaml tags? Yes.

Now what fields does `Topology` have? Check the struct to see if `Links` field exists and whether `clabtypes.Topology` can round-trip links. In `clabernetesRenderTopology`, `Links []*clablinks.LinkBriefRaw`. In `clabtypes.Topology`, Links is probably `[]any` or a custom `LinkDefinition` list. Let me check. If `Topology.Links` unmarshals raw and marshals back, the format may change slightly but semantics preserved.

Let me also check `Topology` struct yaml tags — especially `mgmt` under topology? Let me read the struct.

**G. Config parse of `renderedTopology` vs the file:** Where does `c.Config` come from when LabRuntime is set (since parseTopology is skipped)? Let me check the WithTopoPath option / labruntime parse path. The test uses `c.Config.Topology.Defaults.StartupConfig` — so Config.Topology is populated. Let me find where with lab runtime the Config gets filled — maybe `WithTopoPath` calls `ParseTopologyData` or similar.

**H. Double parse cost:** For each deploy, the topology is unmarshaled 3 times (source, expanded, MapSlice) plus marshal. Topologies are small. P4 at most; actually the double unmarshal of source+expanded is needed for isolation... Actually you could unmarshal once into `source` and then... you need a deep copy to mutate `expanded` without affecting `source`. yaml round-trip is the way. But note the MapSlice unmarshal is a third parse — could reuse? The MapSlice parse is on the original bytes to preserve non-topology keys. That's necessary.

Hmm, but actually there's a redundancy: `expanded` could be built by unmarshaling into it AND `source` — 2 unmarshals — that's what they do. Fine.

**I. `labRuntimeNodeHasStages` guard rationale:** Why not always call GetStages? Because GetStages returns non-nil Stages with InitDefaults even when nothing defined, and serializing that for every node would add empty stages blocks to the definition. The guard is to preserve "no stages" cleanly. But it's checking `definition.Stages != nil` on defaults/kind/group/node. Note: it checks `topology.Nodes[nodeName]` — if node def is nil? `topology.Nodes[nodeName]` exists in map (checked earlier? no). Actually `expandLabRuntimeNodeMagicVars` iterates over `source.Topology.Nodes` keys, so nodes exist in map, may be nil pointers (yaml `r1:` with null value → nil *NodeDefinition). `labRuntimeNodeHasStages` handles nil. OK.

**J. Missing fields in expansion:** What about `Env`? Magic vars in env values? The local path expands magic vars in env? Let me check: in `NewNodeConfig`, env comes from `GetNodeEnv` then `addEnvVarsToNodeCfg` — I don't recall magic var substitution in env. There is `env` with `__clab*` vars? Not supported historically (only `env-files` paths?). Actually magic vars are documented for: node name, lab name, dir, node dir in: startup-config, binds, exec, license, kind-specific config paths, topology name (git vars). Env values — I don't think magic vars are expanded there in the local path. Let me check `GetNodeEnv` usage — probably no substitution. So the new expansion matches local scope: startup-config, binds, volumes, exec, stages exec, kind-specific config. License is missing! `GetNodeLicense` — in the local path, `processNodeLicense` replaces... wait, does processNodeLicense use magicVarReplacer?
